SECTION 22.  OVERTIME 
Division Chiefs are exempt from overtime.  Overtime rates shall be paid on the following 
work: 
 
A. 
Holiday overtime. 
 
B. 
Ordered duty overtime and hold over overtime. 
 
C. 
Overtime work in excess of 182 hours per twenty-four-day work period. 
 
When computing total hours in a work period, Members shall be given hour for hour 
credit for the following paid leave: 
 
A. 
Bereavement leave. 
 
B. 
Leave due to injury on-duty. 
 
C. 
E.O.D. 
 
D. 
Vacations. 
 
All hours worked up to and including 182 hours in a twenty-four-day work period shall 
be compensated at the base rate for the classification that the Member is assigned to as defined in 
Section 21, Classification of Employment and Basic Wages, and will be inclusive of all regularly 
scheduled education, specialty, and longevity pays.  All other remuneration to the Member such 
as pay for vacation, sick, and holidays shall not be considered as regularly scheduled pay for the 
purposes of this Section. 
 
All hours worked in a twenty-four-day work period in excess of 182 hours will be paid an 
additional one and one-half times the base rate as defined in Section 21, Classification of 
Employment and Basic Wages, and will be inclusive of all regularly scheduled education, 
specialty, and longevity pays.  All other remuneration to the Member such as pay for vacation, 

40 
 
sick, and holidays shall not be considered as regularly scheduled pay for the purposes of this 
Section. 
 
Overtime shall be paid at the rate of one and one-half times the individual’s base rate, and 
will be inclusive of all regularly scheduled education, specialty, and longevity pays, or the base 
rate of the position he or she is filling, and will be inclusive of all regularly scheduled education, 
specialty, and longevity pays, whichever is higher.  All other remuneration to the Member such 
as pay for vacation, sick, and holidays shall not be considered as regularly scheduled pay for the 
purposes of this Section.  All personnel shall be granted their choice of overtime pay or E.O.D. 
compensatory time off at the rate of one and one-half hours off for each hour worked. 
 
If overtime work is required to cover a vacancy in any position, such work shall be 
offered to Department Members from a roster which includes all Members.  The shift roster shall 
be maintained by the Union and monitored by the Fire Chief and/or their designees, and such 
work will be offered in as equitable a manner as possible.  Any position filled pursuant to this 
Section shall be paid at the Member’s base rate or the rate for the position he or she is filling, 
whichever is higher. 
 
Overtime work on a holiday shall be paid at an additional two times the base rate for the 
hours that are on the holiday. 
 
Fire prevention positions will be filled at the discretion of the Fire Chief. 
 
Mandatory call out pay shall be two hours minimum.  After the two hours minimum, one 
to thirty minutes shall be compensated for as one-half hour, and thirty-one to sixty minutes shall 
be compensated for as one hour. 
 
The maximum accrual for E.O.D. compensatory time will be 480 hours.  This maximum 

41 
 
accrual applies to any balance of E.O.D. compensatory time including, but not limited to, amount 
earned from committee work, special projects or overtime, sick leave bonus.  Any E.O.D. 
compensatory time over 480 hours must be taken during the calendar year in which it was 
earned.  No Member will be allowed to carry over a balance of E.O.D. compensatory time 
greater than 480 hours on December 31.  The use of E.O.D. compensatory time cannot create an 
overtime situation.  Members hired after July 1, 2019, shall be capped at a maximum of 240 
hours of E.O.D. 
When Day Personnel are working in an overtime capacity on a platoon shift, their base 
rate, education, specialty, EMS, acting, and longevity pays shall be converted to a 56-hour pay 
rate.  Example: Current base rate multiplied by .754   

42 
 
SECTION 23.  WORKING IN A HIGHER JOB RANK 
A. 
In the event a Member promoted above the rank of Firefighter is absent from their 
normally scheduled shift, a qualified Member will assume those duties for the 
duration of the absence. The assigned Member shall receive all regularly scheduled 
base, education, specialty, and longevity pays that the Member is entitled to. 
Members certified to act shall be paid in accordance with Section 21, “Acting 
Certification Pay”. Members are eligible for one Acting Certification Pay regardless 
of the number of certifications the member obtains. 
B. 
When filling an absence through the acting mechanism, preference may be given 
to retain the acting Member at the station in which they have been assigned or chosen. 
Additionally, a Member’s ranking on the “eligibility list” may be taken into account 
to provide for said Member to act more frequently. Final acting placement for daily 
operations will be at the discretion of the corresponding shift’s battalion chief. 
C. 
Except in cases of overtime, the Member(s) assigned to fill said position(s) shall 
be from the same shift. In the event there are no Members from the same shift 
available to act, the absence shall be covered by a qualified Member using the 
overtime mechanism, with the following additional guideline: 
a. 
Overtime personnel shall be utilized at their present rank first, then at a lower 
rank as needed.  
D. 
Certification eligibility: 
a. 
Members previously certified or who have completed departmental training and 
testing shall be grandfathered. 

43 
 
b. 
All members must complete departmental training and testing as approved by the 
Fire Chief. 
c. 
Firefighters assigned to act in the capacity of Engineer must have completed two 
years of service to the Department. 
d. 
Firefighters assigned to act in the capacity of Lieutenant must have completed 
four years of service to the Department. 
e. 
Engineers assigned to act in the capacity of Lieutenant must have completed four 
years of service to the Department. 
f. 
Lieutenants assigned to act in the capacity of Battalion Chief must have 
completed eight years of service to the Department.   
g. 
Any Member may choose to remove themselves from the acting pool, said 
Member shall not receive any corresponding acting pay referenced in Section 21. 
If the Member chooses to reinstate their name into the acting pool, their 
qualifications and eligibility will be subject to review and approval of their 
departmental training and testing as approved by the Fire Chief. The frequency and 
circumstances surrounding the Member’s choice to remove or reinstate themselves 
may be considered by the Fire Chief when reviewing and approving the Member’s 
reinstatement.  
E. 
In the event a Member occupying a Day Personnel position is absent due to 
illness, injury, disability, or other cause and it is expected that the Member will 
resume the performance of the duties of the position, the Fire Chief may assign 
another Member to the position as an actor.  If the Fire Chief chooses to assign an 
actor, the Fire Chief shall assign the highest ranking Member on the eligibility list for 
the position, established by the Civil Service Commission in its Rules and 
Regulations, who is willing to serve as an actor.  If the eligibility list for the position 
has become exhausted pursuant to Chapter I, Section 3.s., of the Civil Service 

44 
 
Commission Rules and Regulations, the position shall be offered to Members whose 
names appear on a roster established to fill the position. 
F. 
All Members on the acting roster for Day Personnel positions shall have four 
years of employment with the Department and possess a Class B driver’s license.  In 
the absence of an established acting roster, the Fire Chief may assign a Member to fill 
the position by temporary assignment.  Any Member shall be placed on or removed 
from a roster by notifying the Fire Chief in writing. 
G. 
Light duty, as defined in Section 27 of this Agreement, will not be considered 
working in a higher job rank. 
H. 
A temporary assignment, if assigned will adhere to Section 2 of this agreement, 
with the following conditions: 
a. If an assigned Member’s work schedule is modified, Section 24 of this agreement 
will be followed to convert pay and benefit hours for the duration of the 
assignment.  
b. A Member shall not be converted to a lower rank or classification in the event of a 
temporary assignment. 
c. The Member will continue to receive all specialty, education, longevity, acting 
and other pays the Member is entitled to for the duration of the assignment. 
 
 

45 
 
SECTION 24.  CONVERSION OF BENEFIT HOURS 
 
A. 
Members who move from a Platoon Personnel rank or position to a Day 
Personnel rank or position, and Members who move from a Day Personnel rank or position to a 
Platoon Personnel rank or position, pursuant to promotion, transfer, temporary assignment, or 
Light Duty will be subject to benefit conversion.  The benefit conversion will be calculated under 
the following formulas: 
1. 
Platoon to Day:  Divide the number of hours worked while on Days 
divided by hours worked on Platoon per month.  Conversion factor shall be 0.754 
(173.333/229.833) = 0.754. 
Example:  A Platoon Personnel Member has accrued 200 hours times 0.754 for a 
total of 150.8 hours (200 X 0.754) = 150.8. 
2. 
Day to Platoon:  Divide the number of hours worked while on Platoon 
divided by hours worked on Days.  Conversion factor shall be 1.325 (229.833/173.333) = 
1.325. 
Example:  A Day Personnel Member has accrued 100 hours times 1.325 for a 
total of 132.5 hours (100 X 1.325) = 132.5.   
 
B. 
These calculations shall apply to conversions of vacation and sick hours. 
 
C. 
Members with less than thirty days in assignment shall not be covered. 

46 
 
SECTION 25.  WELLNESS/FITNESS PROGRAM 
 
The City agrees to provide an annual physical for all Members per National Fire 
Protection Association 1582 guidelines.  In addition, specialty team members will receive any 
additional testing as required by the standard. 
 
Furthermore, the City and the Union agree to adopt and promote the International 
Association of Fire Chief’s International Association of Fire Fighters Peer Fitness and Wellness 
Program. 
 
This benefit does not constitute a prevailing right. 
 

47 
 
SECTION 26.  VACATIONS/E.O.D. 
 
A. 
Annual vacations shall be determined for the period from January 1 to December 
31 of each year based on the years of service completed prior to January 1 of each year. 
 
B. 
All vacation time shall be accrued. 
 
C. 
All rosters shall be made up prior to January 1 of each year in which the vacations 
are to be taken. 
 
D. 
All Platoon Personnel shall choose their vacations on their respective shift, 
according to their seniority on the job. 
 
E. 
A separate vacation roster shall be maintained for each shift. 
 
F. 
A maximum of five slots may be available at any time, four vacation or E.O.D. 
slots and one E.O.D. only slot, except for override vacations due to transfers or approvals from 
the Fire Chief. 
 
G. 
Vacations shall be picked by seniority of the Shift.  The Senior Member shall 
make one pick of consecutive dates or one individual date.  The next Senior Member shall make 
one pick of consecutive dates or one individual date.  The sequence shall continue by seniority 
until the entire Shift has picked.  The sequence shall start over by seniority and continue until all 
Members have selected their picks from available dates. Requests for changes to vacations or 
E.O.D. shall be made to the Battalion Chief of the Shift and shall be awarded on a first come first 
serve basis. 
 
H. 
If, after sick call, there are additional openings that do not affect minimum 
staffing, Members may request “Day of Vacation or E.O.D.” as long as the additional slots do 
not create overtime.  Openings shall be awarded on a first come first serve basis. 

48 
 
 
 
I. 
Day Personnel shall be carried on a separate vacation roster from the Platoon 
Personnel, and they may split vacations. 
 
J. 
During the term of this Agreement, a Member’s vacation or balance thereof shall 
be rescheduled into available vacation slots due to promotion and demotion.  If a Member is 
involuntarily moved, that Member retains their slot at their new assignment.  In the event a 
Member is involuntarily moved, and a slot is vacated by any personnel in the four member 
group, that slot shall be considered vacated and the maximum of four Members off will take 
effect.  In the event there are no vacation slots available, only then will a Member be allowed to 
carry their vacation beyond the maximum four persons off at any one time. 
 
K. 
All Platoon Personnel shall be entitled to a vacation with full pay and all benefits 
as follows: 
After successful completion of the fire academy, a pro-rated amount of 24-hour shifts at 
.8333 shifts per each full month of continuous service to be taken in the following year; 
After one year of completed service, ten 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty; 
After five years of completed service, eleven 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty; 
After ten years of completed service, twelve 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty; 
After fifteen years of completed service, thirteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of 
duty; 
After twenty years of completed service, fourteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of 
duty; 
After twenty-five years of completed service, fifteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of 

49 
 
duty; 
After thirty years of completed service, sixteen 24-hour regularly assigned tours of duty. 
 
L. 
All Day Personnel shall be entitled to a vacation with full pay and all benefits as 
follows: 
 
After one year of completed service, 116 hours; 
 
After five years of completed service, 140 hours; 
 
After ten years of completed service, 164 hours; 
 
After fifteen years of completed service, 188 hours; 
 
After twenty years of completed service, 212 hours; 
 
After twenty-five years of completed service, 236 hours; 
 
After thirty years of completed service, 260 hours. 
 

50 
 
SECTION 27.  SICK LEAVE 
 
All Members of the Department shall earn accumulative sick leave at the rate of one shift 
for each full month completed until a maximum of sixty working shifts of sick leave have been 
accrued.  All sick leave not in excess of sixty shifts, which has been accrued by Members of the 
Department at the date of this Agreement, shall be considered to be vested and shall continue in 
effect.  At the time of separation from the Department, Members with at least four years of 
continuous service shall be paid fifty percent of their accrued sick leave up to a maximum of 240 
hours at their regular base rate computed on 2,080 annual hours not to exceed thirty 8-hour 
shifts.  Sick leave is hereby defined to mean absence from post of duty of a Member because of 
personal illness or injury of the Member.  Sick leave may be used for attendance upon an 
immediate family member due to illness, injury, or accident up to a maximum of three sick leave 
shifts per year. 
 
Sick leave shall not be considered as a privilege which a Member may use at his or her 
discretion but shall be allowed only in case of necessity and actual sickness or disability of the 
Member or immediate family member. 
 
A. 
Beginning January 1, 2014, all Members who take ninety-six hours or less of sick 
leave for a calendar year shall be paid a cash bonus or E.O.D. compensatory time as set forth 
herein. 
 
B. 
All Members shall be eligible for up to ninety-six hours of pay or E.O.D. 
compensatory time when they use less than ninety-six hours of sick leave in a calendar year.  For 
every hour of sick leave used, the pay or E.O.D. compensatory time shall be reduced by one hour 
from the possible ninety-six hours.  Members shall only be able to take up to forty-eight hours of 

51 
 
pay.  All hours earned over forty-eight shall be taken as E.O.D. compensatory time. 
 
C. 
If the Member takes any of the first forty-eight hours as E.O.D. compensatory 
time, up to forty-eight of those hours can be banked for future use.  All of the hours earned above 
forty-eight shall be used on or before December 31 of the year in which they were awarded or 
they will be lost. 
 
D. 
This benefit will be prorated for new Members who have been employed for more 
than six months but less than one year. 
 
E. 
After two consecutive working shifts off-duty from illness or injury, a doctor’s 
certificate of examination shall be required if requested by the Fire Chief covering the period of 
sickness or disability.  In addition to the above, a Member may be required to provide a doctor’s 
certificate of examination in the event sick leave is taken for the shift immediately preceding or 
following a vacation period.  Any examination as ordered shall be paid for by the City.  Upon 
presentation of satisfactory information, the Fire Chief may extend the sick leave or waive the 
doctor’s excuse requirement.  If an extension is refused, an appeal may be made to the Mayor. 
 
F. 
Members may, at any time during the calendar year, donate up to one hundred 
twenty hours of their unused sick leave into the Department Sick Leave Bank or the City Sick 
Leave Bank.  A maximum amount of six hundred 24-hour shifts will be accumulated in the 
Department Sick Leave Bank.  Members will be allowed to use sick leave from the Department 
Sick Leave Bank only when all personal sick leave benefits have been used.  Written application 
for the use of the Department Sick Leave Bank must be made to a Sick Leave Bank committee 
consisting of the Union president or his or her designee, the Fire Chief, and the Mayor or his or 
her designee, within ten business days prior to the date the sick leave is needed.  The committee 

52 
 
may, at its discretion, request a doctor’s certificate of examination before granting use of the 
Department Sick Leave Bank.  Denial of a sick leave request is not subject to appeal or 
grievance.  Members will continue to accrue benefits during the usage of sick leave from the 
Department Sick Leave Bank. 
 
G. 
Upon separation of employment with the City, Members may donate the balance 
of uncompensated hours to the City Sick Leave Bank. 
 
H. 
Members who are recuperating from an injury or illness may return to duty to 
perform work (light duty) consistent with their abilities as determined by the Member’s 
physician.  The Member shall, upon request, furnish the City with a physician’s statement 
specifying the type of work the Member may perform.  The statement shall specify medically 
necessary restrictions, if any.  The Fire Chief shall place the Member in a position which 
contributes in a meaningful and identifiable way to the goals and needs of the Department. 
 
I. 
Any Member who is injured in the discharge of his or her duties shall file such 
reports of accident disability or injury, as shall be required to secure the payment of benefits 
under the Wyoming Worker’s Compensation Act to the injured Member.  While injured, said 
Member shall use his or her accrued sick leave and once exhausted may use the Department Sick 
Leave Bank until return to duty or pension disability is received.  If the Member receives 
temporary total disability payments under the Worker’s Compensation Act, the Member shall 
transfer those monies to the City. 
 
J. 
Claims for medical or hospital charges, drugs, and other compensable medical 
services shall be filed with the Worker’s Compensation Division.  If a Member becomes ill or 
disabled prior to his or her regularly scheduled vacation, the Member and Fire Chief, by mutual 

53 
 
agreement, shall re-schedule the vacation for said Member into available vacation slots, if 
possible. 
 
K. 
Sick leave will be charged by the hour, one hour for every hour off.  In the event 
that a Member returns to work after being on disability pension, said Member shall return to the 
rank and position he or she held previous to the disability without loss of seniority if return to 
work is within eighteen months. 
 
 

54 
 
SECTION 28.  BEREAVEMENT LEAVE 
 
In the case of death within the immediate family of any Member of the Department, such 
Member shall be granted leave with pay. 
 
A Member who is assigned to a Platoon Personnel position may have three shifts of leave 
and a Member assigned to a Day Personnel may have four shifts of leave.  Under extenuating 
circumstances, additional leave may be granted by the Fire Chief. 
 
The Fire Chief may, in the exercise of discretion, grant bereavement leave upon the death 
of a person other than the Member’s immediate family. 
 

55 
 
SECTION 29.  LEAVE OF ABSENCE 
 
Any Member of the Department may be granted a Leave of Absence without pay for a 
period not to exceed three months.  The request for such leave shall be made in writing to the 
Fire Chief not less than ten days before such leave would begin, and is subject to the Fire Chief 
and the Mayor’s approval.  The Fire Chief may also place a Member on a leave of absence with 
pay. 
 
No Member shall be granted a leave of absence to work in another job full time or be 
newly employed and receive benefits from another employer. 
 

56 
 
SECTION 30.  MEDICAL, DENTAL, AND LIFE INSURANCE 
 
Except as provided in this Section, the City shall pay the premium cost for Health, 
Dental, and Life Insurance under “The City Employees’ Insurance Contract,” for those Members 
desiring to obtain such coverage.  If the City offers more than one health insurance plan to City 
employees, Members shall be offered the plan providing more extensive benefits.  Each 
participant in the City health insurance program shall contribute at the same rate as other City 
employees.  Dental insurance coverage shall be provided for the Member.  Dental insurance 
coverage for the Member’s family is available at the Member’s expense. 
 
A. 
Members shall be entitled to insure their dependents under the plans offered by 
the City.  An eligible dependent is defined as a spouse and children up to the age of 26, whether 
married or unmarried.  Dependent children age 26 or older may be eligible to remain insured if a 
physician certifies that the child cannot support himself or herself because of a disability which 
occurred before he or she reached the age of 26. 
 
B. 
Any Member with dependent coverage may add new dependent children at any 
time.  However, the application for coverage must be made within thirty days of the birth, 
placement, or adoption of the child. 
 
C. 
The Union shall have the right to appoint one Member to the City Employee’s 
Health Insurance Committee. 
 
The City shall offer to all Members the option of participating in a flexible health benefits 
plan which qualifies as a “cafeteria plan” within the meaning of Section 125 of the Internal 
Revenue Service Code. 
 

57 
 
 
Whenever any Member’s yearly base salary entitles said Member to a higher amount of 
Life Insurance, such Life Insurance increase shall be effective July 1 of each year with a 
maximum of $20,000.00. 
 
The City shall offer Members fifty years of age or older who elect to retire the option of 
maintaining the current health insurance policy at the single rate.  The City shall pay a maximum 
of $300.00 per month toward the single coverage.  The policy shall remain in effect until the 
Member attains the age of eligibility for Medicare or becomes eligible for coverage through 
another employer.  Members hired after July 1, 2013, shall not be eligible for this provision. 
 
If a Member becomes permanently disabled, as defined by the State of Wyoming 
Worker’s Compensation Division, in the line of duty, or dies due to injury or illness incurred in 
the line of duty, said Member and/or his or her eligible dependents shall be offered health 
insurance at the expense of the City.  The City shall pay for health insurance for the disabled 
Member.  Said coverage will remain in effect until the Member and his or her eligible 
dependents become eligible for coverage through another employer or become eligible for 
Medicare.  Coverage shall be equal to or exceed the health insurance available to other Members.  
Subject to the conditions in this Section, this coverage shall be offered the remainder of said 
Member’s life, and to the dependents of said Member as long as they meet the qualifications of a 
dependent. 
 
Eligible dependents are defined as: 
 
A. 
The spouse, as long as he or she does not remarry. 
 
B. 
Children up to the age of 26, whether married or unmarried. 
 

58 
 
 
C. 
Dependent children age 26 or older may be eligible to remain insured if a 
physician certifies that the child cannot support himself or herself because of a disability which 
occurred before he or she reached the age of 26. 
 
The City of Cheyenne may from time to time require verification of permanent disability 
of Member, of employment, or of a dependent’s age or disability. 
 

59 
 
SECTION 31.  UNIFORMS 
 
A. 
Uniform items and their compliance will be agreed upon between the Fire Chief 
and the Union so as to provide the best options for the Members, while being fiscally responsible 
and assuring for the safety of the Members. 
 
B. 
The City agrees to fund uniform purchases for all Members in an amount of Five 
Hundred Fifty Dollars ($550.00) per Member per year.  The offered uniform items will include, 
but not be limited to, American National Standard Institute (ANSI) compliant work boots, station 
pants, long and short sleeve dress shirts, polo shirt, a tie, Job Shirt sweatshirts, sweatpants, 
workout shorts, coat/jacket, cotton T-shirts (blue, red and white), belts, ball cap, winter cap and 
formal Class A dress uniform.  Additionally, flashlights can be purchased from the clothing 
allowance. 
 
C. 
A set selection of items for new recruits will be agreed upon between the Fire 
Chief and the Union, or its designee.  Members are responsible for the reasonable care of his or 
her uniforms. 
 
D. 
Members shall be permitted to carry over unspent funds for the sole purpose of 
purchasing a dress uniform.  Any unspent uniform funds will expire upon the Member’s 
separation of employment with the Department. 
 

60 
 
SECTION 32.  PERSONAL PROTECTIVE EQUIPMENT 
 
The City will furnish and maintain for each Member serviceable structural firefighting 
turnout gear and equipment consisting of one pair of boots, one pair of trousers that includes 
liners and suspenders, one coat that includes liner, one helmet that includes liner, one pair of 
gloves and a flash hood, and one pair of extrication gloves, as needed.  The City will provide 
coat lights on the truck. 
The aforementioned turnout gear shall be NFPA 1971 compliant.  A second set of turnout 
gear consisting of trouser and coat, a second pair of structural firefighting gloves, and a second 
protective hood shall be provided by the City to all members below the rank of Division Chief in 
order to reduce a Member’s exposure to carcinogens after a fire.  Should the Department absorb 
new operations, the Department and the Union will agree upon proper equipment to be issued to 
each Member.  Members are responsible for the reasonable care of his or her equipment. 
 

61 
 
SECTION 33.  UNION DUES 
 
Upon written authorization by a Member of the Department, the City agrees, at no cost to 
the Union or Member, to deduct the sum certified as Union Dues, and the sum certified as PAC 
fund contribution, if any; and deliver the amount collected from each to the Union treasurer in 
their respective amounts.  If a Member does not have a check coming, or the check is not large 
enough to satisfy the sum certified, no collection shall be made from said Member for that 
month. 
 

62 
 
SECTION 34.  MILEAGE ALLOWANCE 
 
If a Member has arrived at his or her assigned station, and is reassigned to duty at another 
station, said Member shall receive a mileage allowance consistent with City policy for that 
transfer and any other transfers during that shift.  The mileage between stations shall be 
determined by the Fire Chief. 
 

63 
 
SECTION 35.  RESIDENCY 
 
Members will be required to be a resident and be domiciled within a sixty mile radius of 
the Cheyenne Fire Rescue Headquarters Station 1, located at 716 West 19th Street, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming.  As used herein, a resident is defined as a person who is domiciled and physically 
resides within the above-described area with the present intent to remain there during the terms 
established above.  Intent may be established by the Member’s physical presence, which is 
ongoing, not temporary.  A postal box alone is not sufficient to establish residency.  Violation of 
this Section may be grounds for termination.  Residency requirements must be met within three 
months of the Member’s date of hire or six months within the discretion of the Fire Chief. 
 

64 
 
SECTION 36.  TIME OFF FOR UNION BUSINESS 
 
A maximum of four Members representing the bargaining agent shall be excused with 
pay to attend grievance hearings pursuant to Section 8, and/or the contract negotiations and labor 
management meetings, provided they are attended during their regular working hours. 
 
 
 

65 
 
SECTION 37.  UNION USE OF CITY FACILITIES 
 
The fire station meeting facilities shall be available for Union meetings.  Attendance at 
such meetings by Members of the Union shall be only during off-duty hours, except for those 
Members who are on-duty at the station where such meeting is held may attend after 5:00 p.m.  
Under no circumstance shall the readiness of the Department be compromised during any 
meeting. 
 

66 
 
SECTION 38.  ALCOHOL AND CONTROLLED SUBSTANCE POLICY 
 
The City and the Union shall abide by the current City of Cheyenne Alcohol and 
Substance Policy. 
 

67 
 
SECTION 39.  FAMILY AND MEDICAL LEAVE POLICY 
 
The City and the Union shall abide by the provisions of the Family and Medical Leave 
Act. 
 

68 
 
SECTION 40.  PREVAILING RIGHTS 
 
Existing working conditions not specifically covered by this Agreement, but established 
through past practice, shall continue in full force and effect and shall not be altered during the 
life of this Agreement except through the mutual concurrence of the parties, except the City may 
properly and equitably exercise its right to make reasonable and prudent changes in operations 
designed to protect the City from liability or improve the service provided by the Department. 
 

69 
 
SECTION 41.  SAFETY AND HEALTH 
 
The City and the Union shall cooperate fully on all matters concerning the safety and 
health of Members of the Department, and the safety, proper maintenance, and repairs of 
Department equipment and buildings. 
 
To ensure that these matters are taken care of in a timely manner, the Fire Chief and the 
Union shall meet within ten days after receiving written notification of the safety and health 
matter.  The Fire Chief will notify and update the Mayor of the matter.  If a resolution has not 
been agreed upon within ten days, the Union shall meet with the Mayor and the Fire Chief. 
 

70 
 
SECTION 42.  LONGEVITY PAY 
 
Each Member who has completed five years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.2796 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.3707 
 
Each Member who has completed eight years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.3962 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.5253 
 
Each Member who has completed eleven years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.5126 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.6796 
 
Each Member who has completed fourteen years of employment with the Department 
shall receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base 
hourly rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.6290 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.8340 
 

71 
 
 
Each Member who has completed seventeen years of employment with the Department 
shall receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base 
hourly rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.7457 
Day Personnel  
 
$0.9887 
 
Each Member who has completed twenty years of employment with the Department shall 
receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base hourly 
rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$0.8621 
Day Personnel  
 
$1.1430 
 
Each Member who has completed twenty-five years of employment with the Department 
shall receive, in consideration of longevity, the following hourly rate in addition to their base 
hourly rate: 
Platoon Personnel 
 
$1.0951 
Day Personnel  
 
$1.4520 
 
Longevity raises shall be effective the first day of the month following the Member’s 
anniversary date of employment except for those Members who began their employment on the 
first day of the month. 
 

72 
 
SECTION 43.  LABOR/MANAGEMENT RELATIONS 
 
There shall be a labor/management committee consisting of up to six Union 
representatives and six City representatives.  The committee shall meet at least quarterly to 
discuss all matters of mutual concern.  Either party may request additional meetings in the 
quarter as needed.  The committee shall have the authority to make recommendations to the 
Union and the City.  Any recommendation by the committee may be rejected by the Union or the 
City during negotiations. 
 
The Fire Chief and Union shall meet regularly to discuss departmental matters and 
continue to improve working relationships. 
 

73 
 
 
The 2022-2023 Collective Labor Agreement between the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, 
and the Cheyenne Firefighters Local Union No. 279, International Association of Firefighters, is 
signed this ________ day of _____________________, 2022. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Patrick Collins, Mayor 
 
(SEAL) 
 
Attest: 
 
_________________________________ 
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
CHEYENNE FIREFIGHTERS, 
 
 
 
 
 
 
LOCAL UNION NO. 279, INTERNATIONAL 
 
 
 
 
 
 
ASSOCIATION OF FIREFIGHTERS 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
__________________________________________ 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Vincent McQueeney, President 


--- END OF FILE ---


# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-11-01_TotalExtraction_59281289.pdf
1 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Jeanie Vetter, PLA, Parks and Greenway Planner  
Subject: Memorandum of Understanding Between Laramie County and City of Cheyenne outlining 
and agreeing that Laramie County will reimburse the City $407,777.80 for one-third of the 
cost to construct the LCCC/Sweetgrass Underpass  
 
Date: 
November 7, 2022 
 
Background:  
The purpose of this MOU is to outline financial reimbursement to the City for construction of 
the Laramie County Community College/ Sweetgrass Underpass in the amount of $407,777.80 
based on a concept memorandum of understanding meeting held on October 25, 2019.   The 
underpass is complete and total cost for the underpass was $1,923.333.40.  WYDOT provided 
$700,000.00 toward construction.  Upon approval of the MOU by both parties, the City 
Treasurer will invoice the County for the agreed amount and place the funds back into the 2017 
6th Penny Greenway Construction account. 
 
Enclosures: 
MOU 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
Between 
LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING AND THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) is by and between the City of Cheyenne, a 
municipal corporation existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming (City), located at 2101 
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, and Laramie County (County), a body corporate 
and political subdivision of the State of Wyoming, located at 309 W. 20th Street, Cheyenne WY 
82001.  The City and County are collectively referred to as “Party” or “Parties.”  This MOU 
shall become effective upon the date of the last signature affixed hereto. 
WHEREAS, the purpose of this agreement is to outline financial reimbursement to the 
City for construction of the Laramie County Community College/ Sweetgrass Underpass in the 
amount of $407,777.80; 
WHEREAS, a concept Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) meeting was held on 
October 25, 2019 and included representatives from WYDOT, Laramie County Community 
College (LCCC), Developer of Sweetgrass (Lummis Family), City of Cheyenne (Engineering 
Dept), and the Laramie County Board of Commissioners; 
WHEREAS, agreement was made that a multi-jurisdictional funding partnership would 
be advantageous; 
WHEREAS, agreement was made to move forward with a Transportation Alternatives 
Grant (TAP Grant) administered by WYDOT, to design and construct the LCCC/Sweetgrass 
Underpass for a below grade, pedestrian, and bicycle connection under W. College Drive; 
WHEREAS, the LCCC/Sweetgrass Underpass is substantially complete with total cost 
for construction at $1,923,333.40; 
WHEREAS, WYDOT has provided $700,000.00 in grant funding; and,  
By Stefanie Boster at 11:12 am, Nov 07, 2022

 
Memorandum of Understanding 
Page 2 of 6 
WHEREAS, Laramie County has agreed to pay one-third of the City’s required match for 
construction totaling $407,777.80. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the Parties agree as follows: 
 
1. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this MOU is to outline financial reimbursement to the 
City for construction of the Laramie County Community College/ Sweetgrass Underpass 
 
2. 
Term.  This MOU shall commence on the last date executed by the duly 
authorized representatives of the Parties to this MOU and shall remain in full force and effect 
until payment has been made to the City Treasurer’s Office in the amount of $407,777.80. 
3. 
General Provisions. 
 
 
a. 
Amendments.  Any changes, modifications, revisions, or amendments to 
this MOU which are mutually agreed upon by the Parties to this MOU shall be 
incorporated by written instrument, executed and signed by all Parties to this MOU. 
b. 
Assignment.  No Party may assign or transfer the responsibilities or 
agreements made herein without the prior written consent of the non-assigning Party, 
which approval shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
c. 
Applicable Law.  The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this 
MOU shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of 
Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over this MOU and the Parties, and the venue shall be in 
the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
d. 
Compliance with Laws.  The Parties shall keep informed of and comply 
with all applicable federal, state, and local laws and regulations in the performance of this 
MOU. 

 
Memorandum of Understanding 
Page 3 of 6 
e. 
Indemnification.  Each party to this agreement shall be responsible for any 
liability arising from its own conduct.  Neither party agrees to insure, defend or 
indemnify the other.   
f. 
Nondiscrimination.  The Parties shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 
1964, the Wyoming Fair Employment Practices Act, Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq., the 
Americans With Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the 
Age Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations 
thereto, and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, 
creed, color, race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy, or qualifying disability 
in connection with the performance under this MOU. 
g. 
Governmental Immunity.  The Parties do not waive governmental 
immunity by entering into this MOU and specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to the Parties as a governmental entities pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other applicable laws.  Designation of venue, choice of law, enforcement 
actions, and similar provisions should not be construed as a waiver of governmental 
immunity. 
h. 
Third-Party Beneficiary Rights.  The Parties do not intend to create in any 
other individual or entity, the status of third-party beneficiary and this MOU shall not be 
construed so as to create such status. The rights, duties, and obligations contained in this 
MOU shall operate only between the Parties to this MOU and shall inure solely to the 
benefit of the Parties to this MOU. The provisions of this MOU are intended only to 
assist the Parties in determining and performing their obligations under this MOU. The 

 
Memorandum of Understanding 
Page 4 of 6 
Parties to this MOU intend and expressly agree that only parties signatory to this MOU 
shall have any legal or equitable right to seek to enforce this MOU, to seek any remedy 
arising out of a Party’s performance or failure to perform any term or condition of this 
MOU, or to bring an action for the breach of this MOU. 
i. 
Force Majeure.  The performance of this MOU by the Parties shall be 
subject to force majeure including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural 
disaster, war or threat of war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized 
strikes, governmental regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by 
the World Health Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government 
authority or health agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-
19, H1N1, or similar infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other 
similar occurrence beyond the control of the Parties, where any of those factors, 
circumstances, situations, or conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or 
unreasonably delay the performance required by this MOU.  This MOU may be cancelled 
by any Party, without liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or 
amounts paid shall be refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written 
notice to the other Party. 
j. 
Severability.  If any provision of this MOU is found to be invalid or 
unenforceable for any reason, the remaining provisions will continue to be valid and 
enforceable.  If a court finds that any provision of this MOU is invalid or unenforceable, 
but that by limiting such provision it would become valid and enforceable, then such 
provision will be deemed to be written, construed, and enforced as so limited. 

 
Memorandum of Understanding 
Page 5 of 6 
k. 
Notice.  All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this MOU 
shall be in writing and given to the Party either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
l. 
Termination.  Any Party may terminate its participation in this MOU, with 
or without cause, by providing thirty (30) days written notice to the other Party. 
m. 
Prior Approval.  This MOU shall not be binding upon the Parties, no 
services shall be performed under the terms of this MOU, and no payments shall be made 
until this MOU has been reduced to writing and approved by all necessary authorities. 
n. 
Entirety of Contract.  This MOU represents the entire and integrated 
agreement between the Parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations, and 
agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
8. 
Signatures.  In witness whereof, the Parties to this MOU, through their duly 
authorized representatives, have executed this MOU on the days and dates set out below and 
certify that they have read, understood, and knowingly and voluntarily agreed to the terms and 
conditions of this MOU.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-11-01_TotalExtraction_83663554.pdf
1 
 
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Jeanie Vetter, PLA, Parks and Greenway Planner  
Subject: MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING BETWEEN THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND SUN VALLEY 
ESTATES LLC OUTLINING RESPONSIBILIIES IN PURSUIT OF A LAND EXCHANGE OF 
APPROXIMATELY 15,375 SQUARE FEET TO FACILITATE BETTER ALIGNMENT OF THE PUBLIC 
GREENWAY SYSTEM AND BETTER SITE LAYOUT FOR PRIVATE MULTI-FAMILY DEVELOPMENT 
 
Date: 
November 7, 2022 
 
Background:  
The purpose of this MOU is to outline responsibilities of both the City and the Developer to 
pursue a land exchange to facilitate a better alignment of the future Sun Valley Greenway 
Connector and better site layout for the Developer of future multi-family apartments.  Once the 
MOU is approved by Council and responsibilities of the MOU are met by both parties, the parties will 
enter into a Purchase, Sale and Exchange Agreement for exchange of lands.  No money will be 
exchanged.  The City has begun the processes of annexation and zoning for the City owned parcel as 
described in the MOU and the items will go in front of Planning Commission on December 5th and will be 
at City Council for 1st reading on December 12th.   
 
Enclosures: 
MOU 
Exhibit A Land Exchange Exhibit 
Sun Valley to East (Kiwanis) Park Corridor Exhibit 
 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
Page 1 of  7 
MEMORANDUM OF UNDERSTANDING 
 
 
This Memorandum of Understanding (MOU) is by and between the City of Cheyenne, a 
municipal corporation existing under the laws of the State of Wyoming (City), located at 2101 
O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001, and Sun Valley Estates, LLC (Developer), located 
at 2116 Pioneer Ave, Cheyenne WY 82001.  The City and Developer are collectively referred to 
as “Party” or “Parties.”  This MOU shall become effective upon the date of the last signature 
affixed hereto. 
 
WHEREAS, the Developer is developing a multi-family residential subdivision to be 
known as Centennial Heritage, 2nd Filing, situated in the City of Cheyenne, County of Laramie, 
State of Wyoming, (herein referred to as “Development”) legally described as follows; 
Lots 8 thru 12, Block 2, Centennial Heritage and 
 
WHEREAS, the City owns developable adjacent property, situated in the County of 
Laramie, State of Wyoming, legally described in Affidavit Recorded at Book 2763, Page 1714 
(correction to Warranty Deed recorded October 27, 2021, in Book 2760, Page 732) as follows:  
A tract of Land situated in the NW l/4 of Section 2, Township 13 North, Range 66 
West of the 6th P.M., Laramie County, Wyoming, more particularly described as 
follows: 
Commencing at the northwest corner of said Section 2; thence 90°12'46", a distance 
of 1602.32 feet to the point of beginning, said point also being the southwest corner 
of Sun Valley Addition Twelfth Filing and the southeasterly corner of Sun Valley 
Thirteenth Filing; thence 235°40'06", along the south line of said Thirteenth Filing, a 
distance of 700.00 feet; thence 239°39'08", a distance of 216.00 feet; thence 
251°35'28", a distance of 216.00 feet; thence 263°44'44", a distance of 216.00 feet; 
thence 270°13'05", a distance of 170.06 feet to the east line of Centennial Heritage; 
thence 180°13'06", along said east line, a distance of 221.65 feet to the northerly 
right-of-way line of the original main line of the Union Pacific Railroad; thence 
northeasterly along said right-of-way line being a curve concave to the northwest 
having a radius of 1709.85 feet, central angle of 42°07'27" (chord azimuth of 
61°03'41"), a distance of 1257.09 feet to the east line of the NW1/4NW1/4 of said 
By Stefanie Boster at 11:21 am, Nov 07, 2022

 
Page 2 of  7 
Section 2; thence 179°24'36", along said east line, a distance of 252.66 feet to the 
northerly right-of-way line of the present Union Pacific Railroad; thence 
northeasterly along said right-of-way line being a curve concave to the northwest, 
having a radius of 3719.69 feet; a central angle of 6°15'44", (chord azimuth of 
58°50'18"), a distance of 406.54 feet; thence 55°42'26", a distance of 90.46 feet; 
thence 325°42'26", a distance of 258.33 feet to the point of beginning., and 
 
WHEREAS, the City Board of Adjustment approved a conditional use regarding 
construction of a multifamily complex on October 21, 2021; and 
WHEREAS, the City and the Developer have both identified positive benefits to both 
parties regarding a potential land change which is generally shown in Exhibit A, which is 
attached hereto and incorporated herein; and 
WHEREAS, the City and Developer believe a land exchange will facilitate better 
alignment of the greenway system and result in better site layout for multifamily development; 
and 
WHEREAS, the City and Developer shall work together to exchange approximately 
15,375 square feet of land; and 
WHEREAS, to facilitate the exchange, various development-related applications are 
required, including but not limited to annexation, rezoning, conditional use, and site plan for 
involved properties; and  
 WHEREAS, pursuit of this land exchange demonstrates a substantial investment in 
establishing the approved conditional use pursuant to section 2.2.1.c.4 of the Unified 
Development Code of the City of Cheyenne; and  
WHEREAS, the City and Developer, once responsibilities of this MOU are met, will 
ultimately enter into a Purchase, Sale and Exchange Agreement for exchange of lands; and, 

 
Page 3 of  7 
WHEREAS, the purpose of this agreement is to outline responsibilities of the City and 
the Developer; 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, the Parties agree as follows: 
 
1. 
Purpose.  The purpose of this MOU is to exchange land that would benefit both 
parties.  No money will change hands. 
 
2. 
Term. This MOU shall commence on the last date executed by the duly 
authorized representatives of the Parties to this MOU and shall remain in full force and effect for 
one calendar year from the date this document is executed by both Parties unless modified or 
renewed in writing and signed by both Parties. 
 
3. 
Payments.  Neither Party shall have any obligation to make any payment of any 
kind to the other Party under this MOU. 
 
4. 
Responsibilities of the Developer.  The Developer shall: 
a. Seek approval of all necessary applications to divide the subject lands to facilitate 
their Development and the conveyance of land.  
 
b. Seek approval of a conditional use permit application based on the revised project 
layout and land areas; along with any and all required development entitlements; 
including but not limited to Site and Landscape Plan(s), Engineering Plan 
Review(s), Subdivision Plat(s) or Replat(s), and Permits or Licenses. 
 
c. Bear all cost associated with the above-mentioned applications. 
 
 
5. 
Responsibilities of the City. The City shall: 
a. Initiate annexation and zoning of the current City owned property to Medium-
density Residential (MR) zoning district and shall bear all costs associated with 
these processes. 
 
b. Design and construct all Greenway elements within the future City owned 
property. 
 
c. Upon completion of both City and Developer responsibilities noted herein, initiate 
an Agreement for the Purchase, Sale and Exchange of Lands and bear all costs 

 
Page 4 of  7 
associated with this process. 
 
 
7. 
General Provisions. 
 
 
a. 
Amendments.  Any changes, modifications, revisions, or amendments to 
this MOU which are mutually agreed upon by the Parties to this MOU shall be 
incorporated by written instrument, executed and signed by all Parties to this MOU. 
b. 
Assignment.  No Party may assign or transfer the responsibilities or 
agreements made herein without the prior written consent of the non-assigning Party, 
which approval shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
c. 
Applicable Law.  The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this 
MOU shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of 
Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over this MOU and the Parties, and the venue shall be in 
the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
d. 
Compliance with Laws.  The Parties shall keep informed of and comply 
with all applicable federal, state, and local laws and regulations in the performance of this 
MOU. 
 e. 
Indemnification.  Developer agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and 
defend the City from and against any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and 
suits, and the cost and expenses incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, 
which may hereafter arise as a result the performance of the Developer’s duties or 
responsibilities contained herein, including death or bodily injury to any person, 
destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) Developer’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) any 

 
Page 5 of  7 
negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by Developer, or its employees, in the 
performance of this Agreement.  Developer acknowledges that it may incur a financial 
obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
f. 
Nondiscrimination.  The Parties shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 
1964, the Wyoming Fair Employment Practices Act, Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq., the 
Americans With Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the 
Age Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations 
thereto, and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, 
creed, color, race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy, or qualifying disability 
in connection with the performance under this MOU. 
g. 
Governmental Immunity.  The City does not waive governmental 
immunity by entering into this MOU and specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et seq., and all 
other applicable laws.  Designation of venue, choice of law, enforcement actions, and 
similar provisions should not be construed as a waiver of governmental immunity. 
h. 
Third-Party Beneficiary Rights.  The Parties do not intend to create in any 
other individual or entity, the status of third-party beneficiary and this MOU shall not be 
construed so as to create such status. The rights, duties, and obligations contained in this 
MOU shall operate only between the Parties to this MOU and shall inure solely to the 
benefit of the Parties to this MOU. The provisions of this MOU are intended only to 
assist the Parties in determining and performing their obligations under this MOU. The 
Parties to this MOU intend and expressly agree that only parties signatory to this MOU 
shall have any legal or equitable right to seek to enforce this MOU, to seek any remedy 

 
Page 6 of  7 
arising out of a Party’s performance or failure to perform any term or condition of this 
MOU, or to bring an action for the breach of this MOU. 
i. 
Force Majeure.  The performance of this MOU by the Parties shall be 
subject to force majeure including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural 
disaster, war or threat of war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized 
strikes, governmental regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by 
the World Health Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government 
authority or health agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-
19, H1N1, or similar infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other 
similar occurrence beyond the control of the Parties, where any of those factors, 
circumstances, situations, or conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or 
unreasonably delay the performance required by this MOU.  This MOU may be cancelled 
by any Party, without liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or 
amounts paid shall be refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written 
notice to the other Party. 
j. 
Severability.  If any provision of this MOU is found to be invalid or 
unenforceable for any reason, the remaining provisions will continue to be valid and 
enforceable.  If a court finds that any provision of this MOU is invalid or unenforceable, 
but that by limiting such provision it would become valid and enforceable, then such 
provision will be deemed to be written, construed, and enforced as so limited. 
k. 
Notice.  All notices arising out of or from the provisions of this MOU 
shall be in writing and given to the Party either by regular mail or delivery in person. 
l. 
Termination.  Any Party may terminate its participation in this MOU, with 

 
Page 7 of  7 
or without cause, by providing thirty (30) days written notice to the other Party. 
m. 
Prior Approval.  This MOU shall not be binding upon the Parties, no 
services shall be performed under the terms of this MOU, and no payments shall be made 
until this MOU has been reduced to writing and approved by all necessary authorities. 
n. 
Entirety of Contract.  This MOU represents the entire and integrated 
agreement between the Parties and supersedes all prior negotiations, representations, and 
agreements, whether written or oral. 
 
8. 
Signatures.  In witness whereof, the Parties to this MOU, through their duly 
authorized representatives, have executed this MOU on the days and dates set out below and 
certify that they have read, understood, and knowingly and voluntarily agreed to the terms and 
conditions of this MOU.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2022-11-01_TotalExtraction_81158120.pdf
APPROVED 
By Stefanle Boster at 5:00 pm, Nov 07, 2022 J 
2022 BYRNE JUSTICE ASSISTANCE GRANT SUBGRANTEE AGREEMENT 
BETWEEN LARAMIE COUNTY AND THE CITY OF CHEYENNE 
THIS 2022 BYRNE JUSTICE ASSISTANCE AGREEMENT("Grant Agreement") 
(ALN # 16.738) is made between Laramie County, State of Wyoming ("COUNTY"), whose 
address is 31 0 West J9th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming 8200 I, and the City of Cheyenne 
(SUBGRANTEE), whose address is, 415 W. J8th Street, Cheyenne, Wyoming 8200 l. In 
consideration of the promises and covenants set forth below, the parties agree as follows: 
1) Purnose of Grant Agreement. COUNTY shall provide Byrne Justice Assistance (JAG) grant 
funds to SUBGRANTEE in the amount set forth in Section 3, and SUBGRANTEE shall 
undertake and purchase cameras for crime scene documentation and overtime costs 
associated with community policing efforts in both foot and bicycle patrol. Performance by 
SUBGRA NTEE oft he requirements of this Grant Agreement and compliance with all JAG 
program rules and regulations is a condition to SUBGRANTEE'S receipt of monies 
hereunder. 
2) Term of Grant Agreement and Required Approvals. This Grant Agreement is not effective 
until all parties have executed it and all required approvals have been granted. The term of 
the Grant Agreement is from October 1, 2022 through September 30, 2025 (''Tenn"); The 
Project shall be completed during the Term. No expenditures can occur before executed grant 
award agreement between Department of Justice and Laramie County. 
3) Payment. COUNTY agrees to grant monies to SUBGRANTEE for performance of the 
Project, as invoices are submitted for work done in connection with the Project, completed in 
accordance with the requirements of this Agreement. The total payment to SUBGRANTEE 
under this Grant Agreement shall not exceed $54,443.00 ("Grant Award"). Payment will be 
made following SUBG RANTEE'S delivery to COUNTY of invoices detailing services 
performed in connection with the Project in a form satisfactory to COUNTY. 
4) Responsibilities of Grantee Regarding the Project. In undertaking and completing the Project, 
the SUBGRANTEE further agrees as follows: 
a) Professional Services. The SU BG RA NTEE agrees to perform all aspects of the Project in 
a professional manner and in accordance with the degree of care, competence and skills 
that would be exercised by a SUBGRANTEE under similar circumstances, to the 
satisfaction of the COUNTY. 
b) Procurement and Administrative Regulations. SUBGRANTEE agrees to comply with 
federal procurement and administrative regulations as stated in 2 C.F.R. § 215, Uniform 
Administrative Requirements for Grants and Agreements with Institutions of Higher 
Education, Hospitals and Other Nonprofit Organizations: as enacted in regulations by the 
U.S. Department of Health and Human Services. 
c) Compliance with Laws. In the interpretation, execution, administration and enforcement 
of this Grant Agreement, SUBGRANTEE agrees to comply with all applicable state and 
federal laws, rules, and regulations, including but not limited to: 

i) 
SU BGRANTEE agrees to comply with all federal requirements governing grant 
agreements that are applicable, including but not limited to 2 C.F.R. § 230; Cost 
Principles for Non Profit Organizations; and OMB Circular A-I33 AuditsofState 
and Local Governments. The Single AuditActof i 984, 31 U.S.C. §§ 7501 -7 further 
defines auditing responsibilities and SUBGRANTEE agrees to comply therewith. 
ii) SUBGRANTEE further covenants that the Project will be conducted and 
administered in conformity with the Civil Rights Act of 1964,42 U.S.C. § 2000 et 
seq. and the Fair Housing Act, 42 U .S.C.§ 360 I et seq. and that it will affmnatively 
further fair housing. 
iii) SUBGRANTEE shall comply with "Equal Opportunity in Federal Employment", 
Exec. Order No. ll , 246, 30 Fed. Reg. 12,319 (I 965) as amended by Exec. Order No. 
11375, 32 Fed. Reg. I4,303 (1967); as supplemented in the Department of Labor 
regulations, 4 1 C.F.R. § 60 (1998), the Civil Rights Act of I964, 42 U.S. C. § 2000 et 
seq., the Wyoming Fair Employment Practices Act, Wyo. Stat.§ 27-9-105 et seq., 
and any rules and regulations related thereto. SUBGRANTEE shall not discriminate 
against a qualified individual with ad isability and shall comply with the Americans 
with Disabilities Act of 1990, 42 U .S.C. § 1210 I et seq., and any rules and 
regulations related thereto. SUBGRANTEE shall assure that no person is 
discriminated against based on the grounds of sex, race, religion, national origin or 
disability in connection with the performance of this Grant Agreement. 
d) Monitor Activities. The COUNTY shall have the right to monitor all activities of the 
SUBGRANTEE related to this Grant Agreement. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time, to bring experts and consultants on site 
to examine or evaluate completed work or work in progress, and to observe all 
SUBGRANTEE personnel in every phase of performance of work related to this Grant 
Agreement. 
e) Retention of Records. SUBGRANTEE agrees to retain all records related to the Project 
which are required to be retained pursuant to this Agreement or the JAG program rules 
and regulations for three (3) years following COUNTY's date of notice to 
SUBGRANTEE of administrative closeout of the Grant. 
f) Prohibition on Lobbying. In accordance with P.L. I 01-12 1, payments made from a 
federal grant shall not be utilized by the SUBG RANTEE or its subcontractors in 
connection with lobbying Congressmen, or any other federal agency in connection with 
the award of a federal grant, contract, cooperative agreement or loan. 
g) Reporting. Within 15 calendar days at the conclusion of each calendar quarter during the 
Term of this Grant Agreement, SUBGRANTEE shall furnish COUNTY with a report of 
activities occurring under grant. 
h) Suspension and Debarment. By signing this agreement, SU BGRANTEE certifies that 
neither it nor its principals are presently debarred, suspended, proposed ford ebarment, 
declared ineligible, or voluntarily excluded from participation in this transaction nor from 
federal financial or nonfinancial assistance, nor are any of the participants involved in the 
2 

execution of this agreement suspended, debarred or voluntarily excluded by any federal 
department or agency in accordance with Executive Order 12549 (Debarment and 
Suspension) and CFR 44 Part 17, or are on the disbarred vendors list. Further, 
SUBGRANTEE agrees to notify agency by certified mail should it or any of its agents 
become debarred, suspended, or voluntarily excluded during the term of this agreement. 
i) 
Federal Aud it Requirements. SUBGRANTEE agrees that if it expends an aggregate 
amount of seven hundred fifty thousand dollars ($750,000) or more in federal fund s 
during its fiscal year, it must undergo and organization-wide fmancial and compliance 
single audit. SUBGRANTEEagrees to comply with the audit requirements ofthe U.S. 
General Accounting Office Government Auditing Standards and OMB Circular A-133, 
Audits and States, Local Governments, and Non-Profit Organizations. If findings are 
made which cover any part of this award, SUBGRANTEE shall provide one copy of the 
audit report to COUNTY and require the release of the audit report by its auditor to be 
held until adjusting entries are disclosed and made to COUNTY records. 
5) Responsibilities of County. COUNTY will, at its discretion, assist in providing 
SUBGRANTEE access to information, includ ing without limitation providing 
SUBGRANTEE with information concerning JAG program requirements, rules and 
regulations and other statutes and regulations referred to herein, and will cooperate with 
SUBGRANTEE whenever possible. COUNTY shall have no obligations, other than those 
specifically set forth herein, regarding the Project or its performance. 
6) Special Provisions. 
a) Limitation on Payments. COUNTY's obligation to pay SU BG RANTEE for Project 
activities rendered pursuant to this Grant Agreement is conditioned upon the availability 
of state or federal government funds that are allocated to pay SUBGRANTEE hereunder. 
l f grant agreement monies are not allocated and available for COUNTY to pay 
SUBGRANTEE for the performance of the Project, COUNTY may terminate this Grant 
Agreement at any time in its discretion without further liability or obligation hereunder. 
COUNTY shall notify SUBGRANTEE at the earliest possible time if this Grant 
Agreement will or may be affected by a shortage or unavailability of funds. No liability 
shall accrue to COUNTY in the event termination of this Grant Agreement occurs or this 
Grant Agreement is affected in any other way by a lack of funds. COUNTY shall not be 
obligated or liable for any future payments due or promised hereunder or for any 
damages to SUBGRANTEE or any other person or entity as a result of termination under 
this section. 
b) No Finder's Fees. No fmder's fee, employment agency fee, broker fee or other such fee 
related to this Grant Agreement shall be paid by either party. 
c) Office Space. SU BGRANTEE will not include charges or seek reimbursement in any 
invoice submitted to COUNTY for office or building space of any kind obtained by 
SUBGRANTEE for the performance of the Project. SUBGRANTEE will make no charge 
for office or building space unless specific provisions are included for such in this Grant 
3 

Agreement. Under no circumstances will SUBGRANTEE be allowed to purchase office 
equipment with fund s received through this Grant Agreement. 
d) Minority Business Enterprise. SUBGRANTEE is strongly encouraged to actively 
promote and encourage maximum participation of Minority Business Enterprises (MBE) 
as sources of supplies, equipment, construction and services in connection with 
perfonnance ofthe Project. 
e) Budget Transfer Limitation. SUBGRANTEE agrees it will not exceed any of the line 
item totals listed on Attachment A by more than twenty percent (20%) without prior 
approval from COUNTY. Such changes will not result in any change in the total Project 
costs, or a change in the Grant amount. 
7) Default and Remedies. In the event SUBGRANTEE defaults or is deficient in the 
perfonnance of any term of this Grant Agreement or any requirements of the JAG program 
rules and regulations, then COUNTY and/or Wyoming Department of Family Services shall 
have the right to exercise all remedies provided by law or in equity, including without 
limitation: 
a) Immediately tenninating this Grant Agreement without further liability or obligation of 
COUNTY; 
b) Issuing a letter of warning advising SU BGRANTEE of the deficiency and putting the 
GRANTEE on notice that add itional action will be taken if the deficiency is not corrected 
or is repeated; 
c) Recommending, or requesting S U BG RA NTEE to submit proposals for corrective 
actions, including the correction or removal of the causes of the deficiency; 
d) Advising SUBGRANTEE that a certification will no longer be acceptable and that 
add itional assurances will be required in such fonn and detail as COUNTY and 
Wyoming Department ofFamily Services may require. 
e) Advising SU BGRANTEE to suspend disbursement of funds for the deficient activity; 
f) Advising SUBGRANTEE to reimburse any amounts improperly expended and 
reprogram the use of the fund s in accordance with applicable requirements; 
g) Changing the method of payment to SUBGRANTEE; and/or 
h) Reducing, withdrawing, or adjusting the amount of the Grant. 
8) General Provisions. 
a) Amendments. Any changes, mod ifications, revisions or amendments to this Grant 
Agreement which are mutually agreed upon in writing by the parties hereto shall be 
incorporated by written instrument, signed by all parties to this Grant Agreement.

b) Applicable LawNenue. The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Grant 
Agreement shall be governed by the laws of the State of Wyoming. The Courts of the 
State of Wyoming shall have jurisdiction over this Grant Agreement and the parties, and 
venue for any action shall be in the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
c) Assignment. Neither party shall assign or otherwise transfer any of the rights or delegate 
any of the duties set forth in this Grant Agreement without the prior written consent of 
the other party. In the event there is a sub-grant(s) under this Grant Agreement,

grant agreement awarded and shall make such provisions binding on each sub-grantee as 
if it were the SUBGRANTEE hereunder. SUBGRANTEE shall not use this Grant 
Agreement, or any portion thereof, for collateral for any fmancial obligation, without the

funding, either administrative or program dollars, due to SUBGRANTEE'S failure to 
comply with this Agreement and all state or federal DOJ requirements. COUNTY shall 
notify SUBGRANTEE of any state or federal determination of noncompliance. 
e) Attorneys' Fees. I fCOUNTY has to enforce this Grant Agreement as a result of a default 
in the performance of this Grant Agreement, COUNTY shall be entitled to its reasonable

may receive or have access to as a result of its performance under this agreement may be

have access to all confidential information in accordance with the requirements of state 
and federal laws and regulations. Any other parties will be granted access to confidential 
information only after complying with the requirements of state and federal laws and 
regulations pertaining to such access. Nothing herein shall prohibit the disclosure of 
information in summary form, including the publishing of reports of services provided in 
this Grant Agreement, so long as the identity of the client remains confidential and all 
other requirements of law or regulation are met. 
g) Conflict of Interest: The SUBGRANTEE and COUNTY confirm that, to their 
knowledge, no COUNTY employee has any personal or beneficial interest whatsoever in 
the services described herein. No staff member ofthe SUBGRANTEE, compensated 
either partially or wholly with funds from this Agreement, shall engage in any conduct or 
activity that would constitute a conflict of interest relative to this Agreement.

RESOLUTION NO. 
CONSIDERATION OF A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE RATIFIED ACCEPTANCE OF A GRANT AWARD 
FROM THE DEPARTMENT OF JUSTICE FOR A FY2022 EDWARD BYRNE MEMORIAL JUSTICE ASSISTANCE 
GRANT IN THE AMOUNT OF $68,074.00, ON BEHALF OF THE GOVERNING BODY OF LARAMIE COUNTY, 
WYOMING. 
FOR THE PURPOSE OF: FUNDS WILL BE USED JOINTLY BY THE LARAMIE COUNTY SHERIFF'S 
DEPARTMENT AND THE CITY OF CHEYNNE POLICE DEPARTMENT FOR EQUIPMENT, OVERTIME, and 
NIBRS COMPLIANCE (City $54,443.00 and County $13,631.00). 
WITNESSETH 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of Laramie County desires to participate in the DEPARTMENT OF 
JUSTICE EDWARD BYRNE MEMORIAL JUSTICE ASSISTANCE PROGRAM by sponsoring this grant award to 
assist in financing this project; and 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of Laramie County has been provided with preliminary cost 
estimates and information on this project; and 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF LARAMIE COUNTY that a 
grant award in the amount of $68,074.00 be accepted from the Department of Justice for assistance in 
funding the Laramie County Sheriffs Department and the City of Cheyenne Police Department. A formal 
Subgrantee Agreement will be executed between the parties. 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that Sandra Newland, or her successor in the position of Laramie 
County Grants Manager, is appointed as agent of the Laramie County Board of Commissioners to 
execute and submit applications and certifications for these funds and to receive funds and implement 
the programs funded under this grant.

V' Award Letter 
September 22, 2022 
Dear Gunnar Maim, 
On behalf of Attorney General Merrick B. Gartand, it is my pleasure to inform you the Office of Justice 
Programs (OJP) has approved the application submitted by LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING for an award 
under the funding opportunity entitled 2022 BJA FY 22 Edward Byrne Memorial Justice Assistance Grant 
Program- Local Solicitation. The approved award amount is $68,074. 
Review the Award Instrument below carefully and familiarize yourself with all conditions and requirements 
before accepting your award. The Award Instrument includes the Award Offer (Award Information, Project 
Information, Financial Information, and Award Conditions) and Award Acceptance. For COPS Office and 
OVW funding the Award Offer also includes any Other Award Documents. 
Please note that award requirements include not only the conditions and limitations set forth in the Award 
Offer, but also compliance with assurances and certifications that relate to conduct during the period of 
performance for the award. These requirements encompass financial, administrative, and programmatic 
matters, as well as other important matters (e.g., specific restrictions on use of funds). Therefore, all key staff 
should receive the award conditions, the assurances and certifications, and the application as approved 
by OJP, so that they understand the award requirements. Information on all pertinent award requirements 
also must be provided to any subrecipient of the award. 
Should you accept the award and then fail to comply with an award requirement, DOJ will pursue appropriate 
remedies for non-compliance, which may include termination of the award and/or a requirement to repay 
award funds. 
Prior to accepting the award, your Entity Administrator must assign a Financial Manager, Grant Award 
Administrator, and Authorized Representative(s) in the Justice Grants System {JustGrants). The Entity 
Administrator will need to ensure the assigned Authorized Representative(s) is current and has the legal 
authority to accept awards and bind the entity to the award terms and conditions. To accept the award, the 
Authorized Representative(s) must accept all parts of the Award Offer in the Justice Grants System 
(JustGrants), including by executing the required declaration and certification, within 45 days from the award 
date. 
To access your funds, you will need to enroll in the Automated Standard Application for Payments (ASAP) 
system, if you haven't already completed the enrollment process in ASAP. The Entity Administrator should 
have already received an email from ASAP to initiate this process. 
Congratulations, and we look forward to working with you. 
Maureen Henneberg 
Deputy Assistant Attorney General 
Office for Civil Rights Notice for All Recipients 
The Office for Civil Rights (OCR), Office of Justice Programs (OJP), U.S. Department of Justice 
(DOJ) has been delegated the responsibility for ensuring that recipients of federal financial 
assistance from the OJP, the Office of Community Oriented Policing Services (COPS), and the Office 
on VIOlence Against Women (OVW) are not engaged in discrimination prohibited by law. Several 
federal civil rights Jaws, such as Title VI of the Civil Rights Act of 1964 and Section 504 of the
